<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that choiceless polynomial time with counting does not capture polynomial time on unordered finite structures, confirming the noncapture conjecture of Blass, Gurevich and Shelah. A linear-consistency query over 𝔽<sub>3</sub> in a fixed binary vocabulary is decidable in polynomial time but not in the full counting formalism.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): The query $Q$ on all finite structures in the fixed vocabulary $\tau$ of eight binary relations is isomorphism-invariant and decidable in deterministic polynomial time, but is not definable in full choiceless polynomial time with counting.
  - Explanation: The separating query is decidable in ordinary polynomial time but cannot be defined by full choiceless polynomial time with counting.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-query-ptime)): The query $Q$ is isomorphism-invariant and decidable in deterministic polynomial time from an ordered encoding of its input structure.
  - Explanation: Consistency is invariant under relabelling, and an ordered encoding permits polynomial-time Gaussian elimination.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-linear-algebraic-logics)): On nonempty finite $\tau$-structures, $Q$ is definable in both $\mathrm{FPS}_3$ and $\mathrm{FPR}_3$. Consequently neither of these logics is contained in $\mathrm{CPT}$.
  - Explanation: Solvability and rank operators express the query on nonempty inputs, giving the stated noncontainments.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-universe-size)): For each $L\ge2$, the size of $A_b$ is independent of $b$ and equals $$\label{eq:universe-size} N_L=\sum_{e\in E}3^{2\|F(e)\|+1} +\sum_{v\in V}3^{2f(v)+d(v)-1}.$$ In particular, $$L^3\le N_L\le (3^{10}+3^{29})L^3.$$ Every vertex has at least one configuration atom.
  - Explanation: The number of atoms is independent of the vertex charges and grows polynomially in the grid side length.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-opposite-answers)): For every $L\ge2$, the structure $A_{b^0}$ satisfies $Q$ and $A_{b^1}$ does not. More generally, $Q(A_b)$ implies $\sum_{v\in V}b_v=0$ in $\mathbb F$.
  - Explanation: Summing the weighted divergence equations forces zero total charge, while zero configurations solve the zero-charge instance.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-h-automorphisms)): For every $b\in\mathbb F^V$, the action just defined is by automorphisms of $A_b$. On its central subgroup $K$, the action adds $k_e$ to each edge coordinate $z_e$ and leaves all face coordinates unchanged.
  - Explanation: The group action preserves all binary relations; central flows change scalar coordinates while retaining face coordinates.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-cochain)): Let $\ell\in\mathbb F^E$ have nonzero curl on exactly $t$ faces of the $L\times L\times L$ box. There is a vertex function $\varphi$ such that $r=\ell-\partial^{\mathsf T}\varphi$ is supported on at most $2Lt$ edges. In particular, if $t=0$, then $\ell$ is a gradient.
  - Explanation: Successive prefix sums subtract a gradient and concentrate the residual cochain on few edges.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-small-orbit-support)): Let $N=\|A_b\|$, let $q\ge0$, and let $x$ be a hereditarily finite object over the atoms of $A_b$. If its $H$-orbit has size at most $N^q$, then $x$ has a $K$-support consisting of at most $$2L(q\log_3 N)^2$$ edge atoms. The support may be empty.
  - Explanation: Commutators and alternating-form dimension bounds turn small orbits into short central supports independently of hereditary rank.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-transitive-support)): Let $\mathcal X$ be an $H$-invariant, membership-transitive finite family of hereditarily finite objects over $A_b$, and suppose $\|\mathcal X\|\le N^q$ for some fixed $q\ge0$. Every object in $\mathcal X$, including every recursive constituent of each of its members, has a $K$-support of length at most $2L(q\log_3N)^2$. All these supports can be padded to the common positive length $$\label{eq:support-length} s=\left\lceil2L(q\log_3N+1)^2\right\rceil+1 =O_q\bigl(L(1+\log L)^2\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: An invariant transitive family gives the support bound to every recursive constituent.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-box-boundary)): Set $c=1-2^{-1/3}>0$. If $\varnothing\ne R\subseteq V$ and $\|R\|\le L^3/2$, then $$\|\delta_G R\|\ge c\|R\|^{2/3}.$$
  - Explanation: Coordinate projections give an edge-boundary lower bound for subsets occupying at most half the box.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-base-equivalence)): For all sufficiently large $L$, the expansions $\bar A_{b^0}$ and $\bar A_{b^1}$, where $b^0=0$ and $b^1=\mathbf1_{v_*}$, satisfy the same $C^M$ sentences.
  - Explanation: Moving a charge defect within the surviving giant component preserves bounded-variable counting formulas.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-partial-flow)): Let $T\subseteq E$ and $\sigma\in\mathbb F_3^T$. There is $k\in\ker\partial$ with $k\|_T=\sigma$ if and only if, for every component $U$ of $G\setminus T$, $$\label{eq:component-balance} R_U:=\sum_{v\in U}\sum_{\substack{e\in T\\e\ni v}} \epsilon_{ve}\sigma_e=0.$$ If an extension does not exist, there is a nonempty failing component with $$\|U\|\le L^3/2,\qquad \|U\|\le(\|T\|/c)^{3/2}.$$
  - Explanation: A prescribed partial flow extends exactly when each remaining component has balanced net flow.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-base-homogeneity)): Let $r\ge1$, and suppose $$\label{eq:homogeneity-width} 7r+7(6r/c)^{3/2}+1\le M.$$ If two $r$-tuples in the same $\bar A_b$ have equal $C^M$-types, then some $k\in K$ maps the first tuple to the second. The corresponding statement for empty tuples holds without any width hypothesis.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently wide counting type detects obstructions to extending a tuple shift to a central flow.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-hf-transfer)): Let $A_0,A_1$ be nonempty finite structures in the same binary relational vocabulary, and let $K_i\leq\operatorname{Aut}(A_i)$ for $i\in\{0,1\}$. Let $s,m,M$ be positive integers satisfying $$\max\{4,m+1\}s\leq M.$$ Assume the following two properties.* 1. *$A_0$ and $A_1$ satisfy the same $C^M$ sentences.* 2. *In each $A_i$, any two tuples of length at most $\max\{2,m\}s$ with the same $C^M$ type lie in the same $K_i$ orbit.* *For $i\in\{0,1\}$, let $D_i$ consist of the objects $x$ in $\mathrm{HF}(A_i)$ such that $x$ and every object in its transitive closure have a $K_i$-support of length at most $s$. Equip $D_i$ with membership, an atom predicate, and the relations of $A_i$, interpreted as false on arguments containing a non-atom. Then $D_0$ and $D_1$ satisfy the same $C^m$ sentences.
  - Explanation: Sentence equivalence and orbit homogeneity transfer counting logic to hereditarily supported objects without a rank bound.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-hf-type-counts)): Suppose $P$ and $P'$ have the same $C^M$ type in two structures chosen, with repetition allowed, from $A_0,A_1$. If $\|P\|+d\leq M$, then for every realized type $\rho$ of length $\|P\|+d$, the numbers of tuples $\gamma$ and $\gamma'$ satisfying $$\operatorname{tp}_M(P,\gamma)=\rho, \qquad \operatorname{tp}_M(P',\gamma')=\rho$$ are equal. In particular a type realized over one prefix is realized over the other. Equal types also have equal types on every projected or reordered subtuple.
  - Explanation: Exact counting quantifiers preserve the number of extensions realizing each joint atom type.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-hf-representation)): Every value $\phi*\alpha$ belongs to $D_i$ and is supported by $\alpha$. Conversely, every object of $D_i$ has such a representation. Moreover, for every $g\in K_i$, $$g(\phi*\alpha)=\phi*(g\alpha).$$
  - Explanation: Forms evaluated on support tuples represent precisely the supported hereditary objects, equivariantly.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-hf-atomic-transfer)): Let $\phi,\psi$ be forms. Suppose $(\alpha,\beta)$ and $(\alpha',\beta')$ have the same $C^M$ type, where the two tuples may be in the same base structure or in different base structures. Then $$\begin{aligned} \phi*\alpha=\psi*\beta &\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \phi*\alpha'=\psi*\beta',\\ \phi*\alpha\in\psi*\beta &\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \phi*\alpha'\in\psi*\beta'. \end{aligned}$$ The atom predicate and all base relations also agree on corresponding form values.
  - Explanation: Matching joint atom types transfer equality, membership and base relations between represented values.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-hf-bijection)): For any represented-alike assignments with $r\leq m-1$, there is a bijection $f:D_0\longrightarrow D_1$ such that adjoining any pair $x,f(x)$ gives represented-alike assignments. In particular $f(x_j)=x'_j$ for every retained position.
  - Explanation: Matching finite stabilizer orbits yields a domain bijection compatible with retained represented assignments.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-size)): For either input, the input atoms, all vertex codes up to halt, and all objects recursively occurring as members of those codes belong to an $H$-invariant transitive family of at most $9B^3$ objects.
  - Explanation: Tagged quotient codes and their recursive constituents fit inside a polynomial-size invariant transitive family.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-state-exactness)): At every stage through halt, $U_j$ selects exactly the vertex codes of $A_j$, and each $R_j$ defines exactly its corresponding binary state relation. The translated halt and output sentences are exact at every stage $j\ge1$ through halt.
  - Explanation: The constructed formulas recover exact quotient states and halt and output tests through termination.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-substitution-width)): Suppose predicates in a relational formula $T$ are replaced by formulas whose free variables are among their displayed argument variables and whose free-variable measure is at most $w$. Capture-avoiding substitution produces a formula of free-variable measure at most $\max\{\operatorname{fw}(T),w\}$. This allows repeated arguments and unused displayed argument variables. Moreover a formula of measure at most $w$ can be renamed to use at most $w+1$ variable names.
  - Explanation: Capture-avoiding substitution preserves bounded free-variable measure and admits a fixed variable-name palette.

```json
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-transitive-support",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal X$ be an $H$-invariant, membership-transitive finite family of hereditarily finite objects over $A_b$, and suppose $|\\mathcal X|\\le N^q$ for some fixed $q\\ge0$. Every object in $\\mathcal X$, including every recursive constituent of each of its members, has a $K$-support of length at most $2L(q\\log_3N)^2$. All these supports can be padded to the common positive length $$\\label{eq:support-length}\n s=\\left\\lceil2L(q\\log_3N+1)^2\\right\\rceil+1\n   =O_q\\bigl(L(1+\\log L)^2\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-243.1"

      "conclusion": {
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **query** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A query on finite relational structures is a property unchanged by isomorphism.
- **choiceless polynomial time with counting** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Choiceless polynomial time with counting, denoted $\mathrm{CPT}$ throughout this paper, operates on the hereditarily finite sets built from the input elements as atoms.
- **coefficient function** ([source](#eq:query-coefficient)): Define, in $\mathbb F$, $$\label{eq:query-coefficient} C(y,a)=\sum_{\substack{x\in Y\\\mathsf I(a,x)}} \ \sum_{\substack{\delta\in\mathbb F\\ \mathsf Z_\delta(y,x)}}\delta \qquad(y\in Y,\ a\in X).$$
- **consistency query** ([source](#eq:query-consistency)): Let $Q(A)$ mean that this system is consistent over $\mathbb F$.
- **box grid** ([source](#eq:face-cycle)): For an integer $L\ge2$, let $G=(V,E)$ be the box grid with $$V=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}^3.$$
- **alternating form** ([source](#eq:edge-difference)): For vectors in $\mathbb F^2$, define the alternating form $$\omega((a,b),(a',b'))=ab'-ba'.$$
- **edge-group product** ([source](#eq:edge-difference)): Its elements are written $(u,z)$, where $u=(u_f)_{f\in F(e)}$, and its product is $$\label{eq:edge-product} (u,z)(u',z')= \left(u+u',\ z+z'+\frac12 \sum_{f\in F(e)}c^f_e\omega(u_f,u'_f)\right).$$
- **central extension product** ([source](#eq:H-commutator)): On the set $H=C_F\times K$, define $$\label{eq:H-product} (a,k)(a',k')=(a+a',k+k'+B(a,a')).$$
- **edge cochain** ([source](#pm-h-16)): An edge cochain is a vector $\ell\in\mathbb F^E$.
- **face curl** ([source](#pm-h-16)): Its face curl is $$(D\ell)(f)=\sum_e c^f_e\ell_e.$$
- **Kuratowski pair** ([source](#pm-h-29)): For objects $a,b$ in the universe of hereditarily finite sets over the input atoms, use the Kuratowski pair $$\langle a,b\rangle=\{\{a\},\{a,b\}\}.$$
- **free-variable measure** ([source](#pm-h-31)): For a formula $\theta$, define $$\operatorname{fw}(\theta) :=\max_{\xi\text{ a subformula of }\theta}\|\operatorname{FV}(\xi)\|.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R-query** [claim](#stmt-prop-query-ptime) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-query-ptime): Relabelling invariance and finite-field Gaussian elimination. -> An invariant polynomial-time decision procedure.
- **R-size** [claim](#stmt-lem-universe-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-universe-size): Count independent face coordinates and solutions of a nonzero divergence equation. -> Equal polynomial-size universes for the charge pair.
- **R-opposite** [claim](#stmt-prop-opposite-answers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-opposite-answers): Use zero configurations and sum weighted divergence equations. -> Opposite query answers.
- **R-action** [claim](#stmt-lem-h-automorphisms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-h-automorphisms): Verify left multiplication preserves incidence and corrected scalar differences. -> An automorphism action.
- **R-sparse** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse-cochain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparse-cochain): Subtract gradients constructed by coordinate prefix sums. -> A sparse representative of its flow functional.
- **R-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-small-orbit-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-small-orbit-support): Use stabilizer codimensions, commutators and alternating-form isotropy. -> Rank-independent central supports.
- **R-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-prop-base-equivalence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-base-equivalence): Move the unmatched charge in the giant component and induct on counting formulas. -> Base counting equivalence for sufficiently large grids.
- **R-homogeneity** [claim](#stmt-prop-base-homogeneity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-base-homogeneity): Detect failing component balance by a bounded-variable formula. -> Sufficiently wide types determine central orbits.
- **R-transfer** [claim](#stmt-thm-hf-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hf-bijection-2): Represent objects by forms, transfer atomic facts and match finite stabilizer orbits. -> Counting equivalence of supported hereditary domains.
- **R-trace** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-size): Encode quotient classes by tagged sets of ordered pairs and count all wrappers. -> An invariant transitive trace family.
- **R-state** [claim](#stmt-lem-state-exactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-state-exactness): Translate cardinality comparisons, generated quotients, payloads and relation representatives. -> Exact state, halt and output formulas.
- **R-width** [claim](#stmt-lem-substitution-width) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-substitution-width): Apply capture-avoiding substitution through fixed interfaces and rename bound variables. -> A program-dependent variable bound independent of stage count.
- **R-separate** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-substitution-width-2): Fix program constants before choosing a sufficiently large grid and compare halt and output sentences. -> Nondefinability in full choiceless polynomial time with counting.
- **R-logics** [claim](#stmt-cor-linear-algebraic-logics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-substitution-width-3): Define the coefficient matrix with numeric tags and use solvability or augmented-rank equality. -> Definability in solvability and rank logic and both noncontainments.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **Q-consistency**: The answer is consistency over $\mathbb F_3$ of the normalization and selected consistency equations.
- **separate-unknowns**: The configuration and edge unknown families remain separate even when their index sets overlap.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **collapse-unknowns**: Identify unknowns indexed by the same input element. -> The resulting linear system changes the query.
- **unique-contribution**: Retain only one relation contribution for an edge pair. -> The coefficient can change when several relations hold.
- **finite-proof**: Use finite test agreement to certify the choiceless lower bound. -> Finite executions do not establish the asymptotic indistinguishability argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The query is consistency of a finite linear system over $\mathbb F_3$; Gaussian elimination decides it from an ordered encoding.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of finite structures: n and eight lists of binary relation pairs.
# PRECONDITIONS: n is a nonnegative integer; every pair has indices in range(n).
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of booleans giving consistency of Q over the field of three elements.
# TIME: Polynomial in n per structure; direct coefficient construction and Gaussian elimination.
# SPACE: Polynomial in n for the augmented matrix and relation tables.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and python-flint fmpz residues modulo three.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise ValueError; failed witness rechecks raise AssertionError.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz
NAMES=('Ed','Cf','EB','VB','I','Z0','Z1','Z2')
def decide(a):
    n=a['n']
    if type(n) is not int or n<0: raise ValueError('universe')
    R={k:set(map(tuple,a[k])) for k in NAMES}
    if any(len(p)!=2 or any(type(x) is not int or not 0<=x<n for x in p) for r in R.values() for p in r): raise ValueError('relation')
    X=[i for i in range(n) if (i,i) in R['Cf']]
    Y=[i for i in range(n) if (i,i) in R['Ed']]
    width=len(X)+len(Y)
    rows=[]
    for t in X:
        block=[a for a in X if (t,a) in R['VB']]
        row=[int(a in block) for a in X]+[0]*len(Y)+[1]
        rows.append(row)
        for y in Y:
            if not any((a,x) in R['I'] and (y,x) in R['EB'] for a in block for x in Y): continue
            coeff=[sum(delta for x in Y if (a,x) in R['I'] for delta in range(3) if (y,x) in R['Z'+str(delta)])%3 for a in X]
            rows.append([(-coeff[j])%3 if a in block else 0 for j,a in enumerate(X)]+[int(z==y) for z in Y]+[0])
    original=[r[:] for r in rows]
    def mod(x): return int(fmpz(x)%3)
    pivot=[]
    h=0
    for j in range(width):
        p=next((i for i in range(h,len(rows)) if rows[i][j]%3),None)
        if p is None: continue
        rows[h],rows[p]=rows[p],rows[h]
        inv=pow(rows[h][j]%3,-1,3)
        rows[h]=[mod(v*inv) for v in rows[h]]
        for i in range(len(rows)):
            if i!=h:
                factor=rows[i][j]
                rows[i]=[mod(v-factor*w) for v,w in zip(rows[i],rows[h])]
        pivot.append(j); h+=1
    if any(not any(r[:width]) and r[-1]%3 for r in rows): return False
    witness=[0]*width
    for i,j in enumerate(pivot): witness[j]=rows[i][-1]
    assert all(sum(c*x for c,x in zip(r[:width],witness))%3==r[-1]%3 for r in original)
    return True
print(json.dumps([decide(a) for a in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

```json
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of finite structures: n and eight lists of binary relation pairs.\n# PRECONDITIONS: n is a nonnegative integer; every pair has indices in range(n).\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list of booleans giving consistency of Q over the field of three elements.\n# TIME: Polynomial in n per structure; direct coefficient construction and Gaussian elimination.\n# SPACE: Polynomial in n for the augmented matrix and relation tables.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and python-flint fmpz residues modulo three.\n# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise ValueError; failed witness rechecks raise AssertionError.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpz\nNAMES=('Ed','Cf','EB','VB','I','Z0','Z1','Z2')\ndef decide(a):\n    n=a['n']\n    if type(n) is not int or n<0: raise ValueError('universe')\n    R={k:set(map(tuple,a[k])) for k in NAMES}\n    if any(len(p)!=2 or any(type(x) is not int or not 0<=x<n for x in p) for r in R.values() for p in r): raise ValueError('relation')\n    X=[i for i in range(n) if (i,i) in R['Cf']]\n    Y=[i for i in range(n) if (i,i) in R['Ed']]\n    width=len(X)+len(Y)\n    rows=[]\n    for t in X:\n        block=[a for a in X if (t,a) in R['VB']]\n        row=[int(a in block) for a in X]+[0]*len(Y)+[1]\n        rows.append(row)\n        for y in Y:\n            if not any((a,x) in R['I'] and (y,x) in R['EB'] for a in block for x in Y): continue\n            coeff=[sum(delta for x in Y if (a,x) in R['I'] for delta in range(3) if (y,x) in R['Z'+str(delta)])%3 for a in X]\n            rows.append([(-coeff[j])%3 if a in block else 0 for j,a in enumerate(X)]+[int(z==y) for z in Y]+[0])\n    original=[r[:] for r in rows]\n    def mod(x): return int(fmpz(x)%3)\n    pivot=[]\n    h=0\n    for j in range(width):\n        p=next((i for i in range(h,len(rows)) if rows[i][j]%3),None)\n        if p is None: continue\n        rows[h],rows[p]=rows[p],rows[h]\n        inv=pow(rows[h][j]%3,-1,3)\n        rows[h]=[mod(v*inv) for v in rows[h]]\n        for i in range(len(rows)):\n            if i!=h:\n                factor=rows[i][j]\n                rows[i]=[mod(v-factor*w) for v,w in zip(rows[i],rows[h])]\n        pivot.append(j); h+=1\n    if any(not any(r[:width]) and r[-1]%3 for r in rows): return False\n    witness=[0]*width\n    for i,j in enumerate(pivot): witness[j]=rows[i][-1]\n    assert all(sum(c*x for c,x in zip(r[:width],witness))%3==r[-1]%3 for r in original)\n    return True\nprint(json.dumps([decide(a) for a in json.load(sys.stdin)]))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | stated | The query supplies an implementable consistency decision procedure for encoded finite relational inputs. Relabelling the input preserves its answer; a deployed interface or benchmark is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a game or simulation contract. The relational query is a consistency property, and the counting comparison in its proof does not supply simulation dynamics. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | An exact consistency checker for the specified query can support inspection of finite relational inputs. Packaging as a diagnostic tool is an inferred use; a supplied tool interface is ABSENT. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | stated | The paper explicitly computes the query coefficients and tests and then decides finite-field consistency by Gaussian elimination. The lower bound concerns choiceless definability and does not obstruct the ordered decision procedure. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Tagged hereditary codes represent quotient vertices without choosing representatives. The whole equivalence class is retained as a payload, and recursive constituents belong to the bounded transitive trace family. A practical storage implementation is ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: an accelerator implementation or hardware performance contract. The established result concerns the specified finite relational query and its logical definability. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: an cryptographic security reduction or protocol. The established result concerns the specified finite relational query and its logical definability. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-01** (formal_scope): The separation concerns the specified invariant query in its fixed binary vocabulary. Blocked use: ['Applying the separating query as a decision procedure for unrelated properties.']
- **limit-02** (formal_scope): Excluding choiceless polynomial time with counting leaves the broader capture question open. Blocked use: ['Concluding that no logic can capture polynomial time on unordered structures.']
- **limit-03** (edge_case): The query treats overlapping index sets as separate families of unknowns and permits simultaneous coefficient relations. Blocked use: ['Identifying the two families of unknowns when their input elements coincide.']
- **limit-04** (edge_case): Repeated equations remain separate equations; an empty system is consistent. Blocked use: ['Adding repeated equations together or declaring an empty system inconsistent.']
- **limit-05** (edge_case): An empty normalization block makes the system inconsistent. Blocked use: ['Dropping normalization rows solely because their blocks are empty.']
- **limit-06** (hypothesis): The stated solvability and rank logic consequence assumes nonempty input structures. Blocked use: ['Extending this stated corollary to the empty input without separate treatment.']
- **limit-07** (formal_scope): The analytic preorder and scalar-difference relations are not supplied to the choiceless program. Blocked use: ['Using the analytic expansion as extra input when interpreting the separation.']
- **limit-08** (hypothesis): The asymptotic comparison fixes the program-dependent support and formula parameters before choosing the grid. Blocked use: ['Allowing these parameters to grow arbitrarily with the grid while invoking the comparison.']
- **limit-09** (nonconstructive): The supported comparison domain is countably infinite although its individual objects are hereditarily finite. Blocked use: ['Treating the whole supported domain as a finite enumerable computation state.']
- **limit-10** (nonconstructive): The domain matching is an external mathematical comparison, without a requirement that a choiceless program execute it. Blocked use: ['Presenting the domain bijection as a choiceless executable algorithm.']
- **limit-11** (formal_scope): The computation translation relies on the cited interpretation characterization as an external input. Blocked use: ['Claiming that the paper independently derives the interpretation characterization.']
- **limit-12** (counterexample_scope): The opposite-answer argument permits arbitrary field-valued configuration weights. Blocked use: ['Replacing linear consistency by selection of a single configuration at each vertex.']
- **limit-13** (formal_scope): A rank-restricted lower bound does not establish separation for the unrestricted counting formalism. Blocked use: ['Using only a fixed-rank obstruction to justify the full separation.']
- **limit-14** (hypothesis): The support-to-set transfer requires both base sentence equivalence and orbit homogeneity, together with its width inequality. Blocked use: ['Invoking hereditary transfer from base sentence equivalence alone.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 243 (`lean/docs/243.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Choiceless polynomial time with counting does not capture polynomial time](../../preprints/Choiceless-polynomial-time-with-counting-does-not-capture-polynomial-time-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Witnessed symmetric choice is strictly stronger than choiceless polynomial time with counting](../../preprints/Witnessed-symmetric-choice-is-strictly-stronger-than-choiceless-polynomial-time-with-counting-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result separates polynomial time from choiceless polynomial time with counting. It gives an explicit query on finite structures with eight relations that is invariant under isomorphism and decidable in polynomial time, but is not definable in the stated hereditarily finite-set language with cardinality and polynomial bounds on stages and intermediate objects. The query applies to all finite inputs without a promise. The separate witnessed-symmetric-choice result is not included.
>
> The formalization proves that witnessed symmetric choice strictly increases the expressive power of choiceless polynomial time with counting. It constructs one fixed sentence with exactly one witnessed-choice occurrence that gives a Boolean answer on every finite input, while no sentence of the original counting formalism defines the same class of inputs.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

universe uA uB uC uF uR

namespace CPTSeparation

abbrev Scalar := ZMod 3

inductive Symbol
  | Ed | Cf | EB | VB | I | Z (δ : Scalar)
  deriving DecidableEq, Fintype

structure Input (A : Type) where
  rel : Symbol → A → A → Bool

namespace Input

variable {A B : Type} [Fintype A] [Fintype B]

abbrev Edge (S : Input A) := {y : A // S.rel .Ed y y = true}

abbrev Config (S : Input A) := {a : A // S.rel .Cf a a = true}

instance (S : Input A) : Fintype S.Edge := inferInstanceAs (Fintype (Subtype _))

instance (S : Input A) : Fintype S.Config := inferInstanceAs (Fintype (Subtype _))

def coefficient (S : Input A) (y : S.Edge) (a : S.Config) : Scalar :=
  ∑ x : S.Edge, if S.rel .I a x then
    ∑ δ : Scalar, if S.rel (.Z δ) y x then δ else 0
    else 0

def incident (S : Input A) (t : S.Config) (y : S.Edge) : Prop :=
  ∃ a : S.Config, S.rel .VB t a = true ∧
    ∃ x : S.Edge, S.rel .I a x = true ∧ S.rel .EB y x = true

def normalized (S : Input A) (lambda : S.Config → Scalar) : Prop :=
  ∀ t : S.Config, (∑ a : S.Config, if S.rel .VB t a then lambda a else 0) = 1

def consistent (S : Input A) (lambda : S.Config → Scalar) (mu : S.Edge → Scalar) : Prop :=
  ∀ (t : S.Config) (y : S.Edge), S.incident t y →
    mu y = ∑ a : S.Config, if S.rel .VB t a then lambda a * S.coefficient y a else 0

def query (S : Input A) : Prop :=
  ∃ (lambda : S.Config → Scalar) (mu : S.Edge → Scalar), S.normalized lambda ∧ S.consistent lambda mu

structure Iso (S : Input A) (T : Input B) extends A ≃ B where
  rel_eq : ∀ r x y, T.rel r (toEquiv x) (toEquiv y) = S.rel r x y

end Input

namespace Hereditary

section

abbrev HF (A : Type uA) := Quotient (@Lists.instSetoidLists A)

variable {A : Type uA} {B : Type uB}

local instance instDecidableEqHF {A : Type uA} : DecidableEq (HF A) := Classical.decEq _

def mk (l : Lists A) : HF A := Quotient.mk Lists.instSetoidLists l

def atom (a : A) : HF A := mk (Lists.atom a)

def isSet (x : HF A) : Bool := Quotient.liftOn x (fun l => l.1)
  (by intro l r h; cases h <;> rfl)

def rawElements (l : Lists A) : Finset (HF A) := by
  classical
  exact l.toList.toFinset.image mk

def elements (x : HF A) : Finset (HF A) := Quotient.liftOn x rawElements (by
  have mem_rawElements (a l : Lists A) : mk a ∈ rawElements l ↔ a ∈ l := by
    classical
    rcases l with ⟨(_ | _), l⟩
    · cases l
      change mk a ∈ (∅ : Finset (HF A)) ↔ False
      simp
    · change mk a ∈ (Lists'.toList l).toFinset.image mk ↔ a ∈ l
      simp only [Finset.mem_image, List.mem_toFinset, Lists'.mem_def, mk, Quotient.eq]
      constructor
      · rintro ⟨a',ha',heq⟩
        exact ⟨a',ha',heq.symm⟩
      · rintro ⟨a',ha',heq⟩
        exact ⟨a',ha',heq.symm⟩
  have raw_mem_congr_right {l r : Lists A} (h : Lists.Equiv l r) (a : Lists A) :
      a ∈ l ↔ a ∈ r := by
    cases h with
    | refl => rfl
    | antisymm hl hr =>
      exact ⟨Lists'.mem_of_subset hl, Lists'.mem_of_subset hr⟩
  intro l r h
  ext z
  induction z using Quotient.inductionOn with
  | _ a =>
    change mk a ∈ rawElements l ↔ mk a ∈ rawElements r
    rw [mem_rawElements, mem_rawElements]
    exact raw_mem_congr_right h a)

instance : Membership (HF A) (HF A) := ⟨fun y x => x ∈ elements y⟩

def ofFinset (s : Finset (HF A)) : HF A :=
  mk (Lists.ofList (s.toList.map Quotient.out))

def rawRank' : {b : Bool} → Lists' A b → ℕ
  | _, .atom _ => 0
  | _, .nil => 0
  | _, .cons' a l => max (rawRank' a + 1) (rawRank' l)

def rawRank (l : Lists A) : ℕ := rawRank' l.2

def rank (x : HF A) : ℕ := Quotient.liftOn x rawRank (by
  have rawRank_lt_of_mem {b : Bool} (l : Lists' A b) (a : Lists A)
      (ha : a ∈ l.toList) : rawRank a < rawRank' l := by
    induction l with
    | atom => simp at ha
    | nil => simp at ha
    | cons' c l ihc ihl =>
      rcases List.mem_cons.mp ha with rfl | ha
      · exact lt_of_lt_of_le (Nat.lt_succ_self _) (le_max_left _ _)
      · exact lt_of_lt_of_le (ihl ha) (le_max_right _ _)
  have rawRank_congr {l r : Lists A} (h : Lists.Equiv l r) : rawRank l = rawRank r := by
    induction h using Lists.Equiv.rec
        (motive_2 := fun l r _ => rawRank' l ≤ rawRank' r) with
    | refl => rfl
    | antisymm hl hr ihl ihr => exact Nat.le_antisymm ihl ihr
    | nil => exact Nat.zero_le _
    | @cons a a' l r heq hmem hsub iheq ihsub =>
      change max (rawRank a + 1) (rawRank' l) ≤ rawRank' r
      refine max_le ?_ ihsub
      rw [iheq]
      exact rawRank_lt_of_mem r a' hmem
  exact fun _ _ h => rawRank_congr h)

end

open Classical Finset

variable {A : Type uA} {B : Type uB}

def double (a b : HF A) : HF A := ofFinset {a,b}

def ordinal : ℕ → HF A
  | 0 => ofFinset ∅
  | i+1 => ofFinset (insert (ordinal i) (elements (ordinal i)))

end Hereditary

namespace Operational

open Classical Hereditary Finset

variable {A : Type uA} {B : Type uB} {R : Type uR} {F : Type uF} {arity : F → ℕ}

local instance instDecidableEqHF {C : Type uC} : DecidableEq (HF C) := Classical.decEq _

abbrev empty : HF A := ordinal 0

abbrev truth : HF A := ordinal 1

def boolean (p : Prop) : HF A := if p then truth else empty

def inputRelation (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (r : R) (x y : HF A) : Prop :=
  ∃ a b, x = atom a ∧ y = atom b ∧ rel r a b = true

def allAtoms [Fintype A] : HF A := ofFinset (univ.image atom)

def unionHF (x : HF A) : HF A := ofFinset ((elements x).biUnion elements)

def uniqueHF (x : HF A) : HF A :=
  if h : ∃ a, elements x = {a} then h.choose else empty

def cardinalityHF (x : HF A) : HF A := ordinal (elements x).card

abbrev Location (F : Type uF) (arity : F → ℕ) (A : Type uA) := Σ f : F, Fin (arity f) → HF A

structure State (F : Type uF) (arity : F → ℕ) (A : Type uA) where
  value : Location F arity A → HF A
  finite : Set.Finite {l | value l ≠ empty}

namespace State

def initial : State F arity A := ⟨fun _ => empty, by simp⟩

def support (s : State F arity A) : Finset (Location F arity A) := s.finite.toFinset

end State

abbrev Update (F : Type uF) (arity : F → ℕ) (A : Type uA) := Location F arity A × HF A

def State.Consistent (u : Finset (Update F arity A)) : Prop :=
  ∀ l x y, (l,x) ∈ u → (l,y) ∈ u → x = y

def State.updatedValue (s : State F arity A) (u : Finset (Update F arity A))
    (l : Location F arity A) : HF A :=
  if h : ∃ x, (l,x) ∈ u then h.choose else s.value l

def State.applyUpdates (s : State F arity A) (u : Finset (Update F arity A)) : State F arity A where
  value := s.updatedValue u
  finite := by
    apply (s.finite.union (u.image Prod.fst).finite_toSet).subset
    intro l hl
    by_cases h : ∃ x, (l,x) ∈ u
    · obtain ⟨x,hx⟩ := h
      exact Or.inr (mem_image.mpr ⟨(l,x),hx,rfl⟩)
    · exact Or.inl (by simpa only [Set.mem_ofPred_eq,updatedValue,dite_eq_right h] using hl)

inductive Control | halt | accept deriving DecidableEq

end Operational

section

open Classical Finset

variable {A : Type uA} {B : Type uB}

local instance Hereditary.instDecidableEqHF_1 {C : Type uC} : DecidableEq (HF C) := Classical.decEq _

def Hereditary.closure (x : HF A) : Finset (HF A) :=
  insert x ((elements x).attach.biUnion (fun y => closure y.val))
termination_by rank x
decreasing_by
  have mem_rawElements (a l : Lists A) : mk a ∈ rawElements l ↔ a ∈ l := by
    classical
    rcases l with ⟨(_ | _), l⟩
    · cases l
      change mk a ∈ (∅ : Finset (HF A)) ↔ False
      simp
    · change mk a ∈ (Lists'.toList l).toFinset.image mk ↔ a ∈ l
      simp only [Finset.mem_image, List.mem_toFinset, Lists'.mem_def, mk, Quotient.eq]
      constructor
      · rintro ⟨a',ha',heq⟩
        exact ⟨a',ha',heq.symm⟩
      · rintro ⟨a',ha',heq⟩
        exact ⟨a',ha',heq.symm⟩
  have rawRank_lt_of_mem {b : Bool} (l : Lists' A b) (a : Lists A)
      (ha : a ∈ l.toList) : rawRank a < rawRank' l := by
    induction l with
    | atom => simp at ha
    | nil => simp at ha
    | cons' c l ihc ihl =>
      rcases List.mem_cons.mp ha with rfl | ha
      · exact lt_of_lt_of_le (Nat.lt_succ_self _) (le_max_left _ _)
      · exact lt_of_lt_of_le (ihl ha) (le_max_right _ _)
  have rawRank_congr {l r : Lists A} (h : Lists.Equiv l r) : rawRank l = rawRank r := by
    induction h using Lists.Equiv.rec
        (motive_2 := fun l r _ => rawRank' l ≤ rawRank' r) with
    | refl => rfl
    | antisymm _hl _hr ihl ihr => exact Nat.le_antisymm ihl ihr
    | nil => exact Nat.zero_le _
    | @cons a a' l r _heq hmem _hsub iheq ihsub =>
      change max (rawRank a + 1) (rawRank' l) ≤ rawRank' r
      refine max_le ?_ ihsub
      rw [iheq]
      exact rawRank_lt_of_mem r a' hmem
  have rank_lt_of_mem {x y : HF A} (h : x ∈ y) : rank x < rank y := by
    induction y using Quotient.inductionOn with
    | _ l =>
      induction x using Quotient.inductionOn with
      | _ a =>
        have h' := (mem_rawElements a l).mp h
        rcases l with ⟨(_ | _), l⟩
        · exact False.elim h'
        · obtain ⟨a',ha',heq⟩ := h'
          change rawRank a < rawRank' l
          rw [rawRank_congr heq]
          exact rawRank_lt_of_mem l a' ha'
  exact rank_lt_of_mem y.property

def Hereditary.familyClosure (f : Finset (HF A)) : Finset (HF A) := f.biUnion closure

end

section

open Classical Hereditary Finset

variable {A : Type uA} {R : Type uR} {F : Type uF} {arity : F → ℕ}

local instance Operational.instDecidableEqHF_1 {C : Type uC} : DecidableEq (HF C) := Classical.decEq _

def Operational.State.critical (s : State F arity A) : Finset (HF A) :=
  s.support.biUnion (fun l => insert (s.value l) (univ.image l.2))

def Operational.State.active [Fintype A] (s : State F arity A) : Finset (HF A) :=
  familyClosure (s.critical ∪ {allAtoms,empty,truth} ∪ univ.image atom)

end

namespace FullCPT

section

open Classical Hereditary Finset

open Operational (State Control Location Update)

local instance instDecidableEqHF {C : Type} : DecidableEq (HF C) := Classical.decEq _

inductive Term (R F : Type) (arity : F → ℕ) : Type
  | var (name : ℕ)
  | ordinal (n : ℕ)
  | atoms
  | app (f : F) (args : Fin (arity f) → Term R F arity)
  | pair (a b : Term R F arity)
  | union (a : Term R F arity)
  | unique (a : Term R F arity)
  | card (a : Term R F arity)
  | equal (a b : Term R F arity)
  | member (a b : Term R F arity)
  | isAtom (a : Term R F arity)
  | input (r : R) (a b : Term R F arity)
  | not (a : Term R F arity)
  | and (a b : Term R F arity)
  | or (a b : Term R F arity)
  | conditional (c a b : Term R F arity)
  | comprehend (name : ℕ) (bound guard body : Term R F arity)

variable {A R F : Type} {arity : F → ℕ}

namespace Term

variable [Fintype A]

def eval (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : State F arity A) :
    Term R F arity → (ℕ → HF A) → HF A
  | .var n, v => v n
  | .ordinal n, _ => Hereditary.ordinal n
  | .atoms, _ => Operational.allAtoms
  | .app f args, v => s.value ⟨f, fun i => eval rel s (args i) v⟩
  | .pair a b, v => double (eval rel s a v) (eval rel s b v)
  | .union a, v => Operational.unionHF (eval rel s a v)
  | .unique a, v => Operational.uniqueHF (eval rel s a v)
  | .card a, v => Operational.cardinalityHF (eval rel s a v)
  | .equal a b, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v = eval rel s b v)
  | .member a b, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v ∈ eval rel s b v)
  | .isAtom a, v => Operational.boolean (isSet (eval rel s a v) = false)
  | .input r a b, v => Operational.boolean (Operational.inputRelation rel r (eval rel s a v) (eval rel s b v))
  | .not a, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v ≠ Operational.truth)
  | .and a b, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v = Operational.truth ∧ eval rel s b v = Operational.truth)
  | .or a b, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v = Operational.truth ∨ eval rel s b v = Operational.truth)
  | .conditional c a b, v => if eval rel s c v = Operational.truth then eval rel s a v else eval rel s b v
  | .comprehend n bound guard body, v =>
      ofFinset (((elements (eval rel s bound v)).filter
        (fun x => eval rel s guard (Function.update v n x) = Operational.truth)).image
        (fun x => eval rel s body (Function.update v n x)))

end Term

inductive Rule (R F : Type) (arity : F → ℕ) : Type
  | skip
  | update (f : F) (args : Fin (arity f) → Term R F arity) (value : Term R F arity)
  | parallel (a b : Rule R F arity)
  | conditional (condition : Term R F arity) (a b : Rule R F arity)
  | forall (name : ℕ) (bound : Term R F arity) (body : Rule R F arity)
  | letValue (name : ℕ) (value : Term R F arity) (body : Rule R F arity)

namespace Rule

def updates [Fintype A] (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : State F arity A) :
    Rule R F arity → (ℕ → HF A) → Finset (Update F arity A)
  | .skip, _ => ∅
  | .update f args value, v => {(⟨f, fun i => (args i).eval rel s v⟩, value.eval rel s v)}
  | .parallel a b, v => updates rel s a v ∪ updates rel s b v
  | .conditional c a b, v => if c.eval rel s v = Operational.truth then updates rel s a v else updates rel s b v
  | .forall n bound body, v => (elements (bound.eval rel s v)).biUnion
      (fun x => updates rel s body (Function.update v n x))
  | .letValue n value body, v => updates rel s body (Function.update v n (value.eval rel s v))

end Rule

structure Program (R : Type) where
  Functions : Type
  finiteFunctions : Fintype Functions
  arity : Functions → ℕ
  body : Rule R (Control ⊕ Functions) (Sum.elim (fun _ => 0) arity)

namespace Program

variable (P : Program R) [Fintype A]

abbrev functionArity : Control ⊕ P.Functions → ℕ := Sum.elim (fun _ => 0) P.arity

abbrev Store := State (Control ⊕ P.Functions) P.functionArity A

def flag (s : P.Store (A := A)) (c : Control) : Prop := s.value ⟨.inl c, Fin.elim0⟩ = Operational.truth

def step (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : P.Store (A := A)) : Option (P.Store (A := A)) :=
  if P.flag s .halt then some s else
    let u := P.body.updates rel s (fun _ => Operational.empty)
    if State.Consistent u then some (s.applyUpdates u) else none

def run (rel : R → A → A → Bool) : ℕ → Option (P.Store (A := A))
  | 0 => some State.initial
  | j+1 => (run rel j).bind (P.step rel)

def active (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (h : ℕ) : Finset (HF A) :=
  (Finset.range (h+1)).biUnion (fun j => ((P.run rel j).toFinset).biUnion State.active)

end Program

end

open Classical Hereditary Finset

open Operational (State Control)

local instance instDecidableEqHF_1 {C : Type} : DecidableEq (HF C) := Classical.decEq _

variable {A R F : Type} {arity : F → ℕ}

section

variable [Fintype A]

def Term.roots (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : State F arity A) :
    Term R F arity → (ℕ → HF A) → Finset (HF A)
  | t@(.var _), v | t@(.ordinal _), v | t@(.atoms), v => {t.eval rel s v}
  | t@(.app _ args), v => insert (t.eval rel s v)
      ((List.ofFn (fun i => roots rel s (args i) v)).foldr (· ∪ ·) ∅)
  | t@(.pair a b), v | t@(.equal a b), v | t@(.member a b), v
  | t@(.input _ a b), v | t@(.and a b), v | t@(.or a b), v =>
      insert (t.eval rel s v) (roots rel s a v ∪ roots rel s b v)
  | t@(.union a), v | t@(.unique a), v | t@(.card a), v
  | t@(.isAtom a), v | t@(.not a), v => insert (t.eval rel s v) (roots rel s a v)
  | .conditional c a b, v => roots rel s c v ∪
      if c.eval rel s v = Operational.truth then roots rel s a v else roots rel s b v
  | t@(.comprehend n bound guard body), v => insert (t.eval rel s v)
      (roots rel s bound v ∪ (elements (bound.eval rel s v)).biUnion (fun x =>
        roots rel s guard (Function.update v n x) ∪
        if guard.eval rel s (Function.update v n x) = Operational.truth then
          roots rel s body (Function.update v n x) else ∅))

def Rule.roots (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : State F arity A) :
 Rule R F arity → (ℕ → HF A) → Finset (HF A)
 | .skip, _ => ∅
 | .update _ args value, v => value.roots rel s v ∪
     (List.ofFn (fun i => (args i).roots rel s v)).foldr (· ∪ ·) ∅
 | .parallel a b, v => roots rel s a v ∪ roots rel s b v
 | .conditional c a b, v => c.roots rel s v ∪
     if c.eval rel s v = Operational.truth then roots rel s a v else roots rel s b v
 | .forall n bound body, v => bound.roots rel s v ∪
     (elements (bound.eval rel s v)).biUnion (fun x => roots rel s body (Function.update v n x))
 | .letValue n value body, v => value.roots rel s v ∪
     roots rel s body (Function.update v n (value.eval rel s v))

end

section

variable (P : Program R) [Fintype A]

def Program.stepRoots (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : P.Store (A := A)) : Finset (HF A) :=
 if P.flag s .halt then ∅ else P.body.roots rel s (fun _ => Operational.empty)

def Program.occurring (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (h : ℕ) : Finset (HF A) :=
 P.active rel h ∪ (range h).biUnion (fun j => match P.run rel j with
   | none => ∅
   | some s => familyClosure (P.stepRoots rel s))

def Program.evaluationAccepts (time space : Polynomial ℕ) (rel : R → A → A → Bool) : Prop :=
 ∃ h st, h ≤ time.eval (Fintype.card A) ∧ P.run rel h = some st ∧ P.flag st .halt ∧ P.flag st .accept ∧
   (P.occurring rel h).card ≤ space.eval (Fintype.card A)

end

def EvaluationDefinable (Q : ∀ {A : Type}, [Fintype A] → Input A → Prop) : Prop :=
 ∃ P : Program Symbol, ∃ time space : Polynomial ℕ,
   ∀ (A : Type) [Fintype A] (I : Input A), P.evaluationAccepts time space I.rel ↔ Q I

end FullCPT

namespace OrderedQuery

section

open Finset

variable {N : ℕ}

abbrev Data (N : ℕ) := Input (Fin N)

end

def indexSymbol (i : Fin 8) : Symbol :=
  if i.val = 0 then .Ed else
  if i.val = 1 then .Cf else
  if i.val = 2 then .EB else
  if i.val = 3 then .VB else
  if i.val = 4 then .I else .Z ((i.val-5 : ℕ) : Scalar)

variable {N : ℕ}

def cellValue (S : Data N) (k : Fin (8*(N*N))) : Bool :=
  let ij := finProdFinEquiv.symm k
  let ab := finProdFinEquiv.symm ij.2
  S.rel (indexSymbol ij.1) ab.1 ab.2

def tableBits (S : Data N) : List Bool := List.ofFn (cellValue S)

abbrev OrderedInput := Σ N, Data N

end OrderedQuery

namespace GaussMachine

abbrev Letter := Option Scalar

def boolCode (b : Bool) : List Letter := [some (if b then 1 else 0)]

end GaussMachine

namespace CounterLang

abbrev Letter := Option Scalar

end CounterLang

namespace TableMachine

open CounterLang

def bitLetter (b : Bool) : Letter := some (if b then 1 else 0)

def tableInput {N} (S : OrderedQuery.Data N) : List Letter :=
 (OrderedQuery.tableBits S).map bitLetter

def code (S : OrderedQuery.OrderedInput) : List Letter :=
 List.replicate S.1 none ++ some 0 :: tableInput S.2

end TableMachine

def Input.ordered {A : Type} {N : ℕ} (S : Input A) (e : A ≃ Fin N) : OrderedQuery.Data N :=
  ⟨fun r i j => S.rel r (e.symm i) (e.symm j)⟩

def OrdinaryPolynomialTime : Prop :=
  ∃ answer : OrderedQuery.OrderedInput → Bool,
    ∃ machine : Turing.TM2ComputableInPolyTime TableMachine.code GaussMachine.boolCode answer,
      (∀ k, Finite (machine.tm.Γ k)) ∧
      ∀ (A : Type) [Fintype A] (N : ℕ) (e : A ≃ Fin N) (S : Input A),
        answer ⟨N,S.ordered e⟩ = true ↔ S.query

theorem main :
    (∀ (A B : Type) [Fintype A] [Fintype B] (S : Input A) (T : Input B),
      S.Iso T → (S.query ↔ T.query)) ∧
    OrdinaryPolynomialTime ∧
    ¬ FullCPT.EvaluationDefinable (fun {_} [_] I => I.query) := by
  sorry

end CPTSeparation

end

end OAI
```

```json
      "statement_lean": "def Hereditary.closure (x : HF A) : Finset (HF A) :=\n  insert x ((elements x).attach.biUnion (fun y => closure y.val))\ntermination_by rank x\ndecreasing_by\n  have mem_rawElements (a l : Lists A) : mk a ∈ rawElements l ↔ a ∈ l := by\n    classical\n    rcases l with ⟨(_ | _), l⟩\n    · cases l\n      change mk a ∈ (∅ : Finset (HF A)) ↔ False\n      simp\n    · change mk a ∈ (Lists'.toList l).toFinset.image mk ↔ a ∈ l\n      simp only [Finset.mem_image, List.mem_toFinset, Lists'.mem_def, mk, Quotient.eq]\n      constructor\n      · rintro ⟨a',ha',heq⟩\n        exact ⟨a',ha',heq.symm⟩\n      · rintro ⟨a',ha',heq⟩\n        exact ⟨a',ha',heq.symm⟩\n  have rawRank_lt_of_mem {b : Bool} (l : Lists' A b) (a : Lists A)\n      (ha : a ∈ l.toList) : rawRank a < rawRank' l := by\n    induction l with\n    | atom => simp at ha\n    | nil => simp at ha\n    | cons' c l ihc ihl =>\n      rcases List.mem_cons.mp ha with rfl | ha\n      · exact lt_of_lt_of_le (Nat.lt_succ_self _) (le_max_left _ _)\n      · exact lt_of_lt_of_le (ihl ha) (le_max_right _ _)\n  have rawRank_congr {l r : Lists A} (h : Lists.Equiv l r) : rawRank l = rawRank r := by\n    induction h using Lists.Equiv.rec\n        (motive_2 := fun l r _ => rawRank' l ≤ rawRank' r) with\n    | refl => rfl\n    | antisymm _hl _hr ihl ihr => exact Nat.le_antisymm ihl ihr\n    | nil => exact Nat.zero_le _\n    | @cons a a' l r _heq hmem _hsub iheq ihsub =>\n      change max (rawRank a + 1) (rawRank' l) ≤ rawRank' r\n      refine max_le ?_ ihsub\n      rw [iheq]\n      exact rawRank_lt_of_mem r a' hmem\n  have rank_lt_of_mem {x y : HF A} (h : x ∈ y) : rank x < rank y := by\n    induction y using Quotient.inductionOn with\n    | _ l =>\n      induction x using Quotient.inductionOn with\n      | _ a =>\n        have h' := (mem_rawElements a l).mp h\n        rcases l with ⟨(_ | _), l⟩\n        · exact False.elim h'\n        · obtain ⟨a',ha',heq⟩ := h'\n          change rawRank a < rawRank' l\n          rw [rawRank_congr heq]\n          exact rawRank_lt_of_mem l a' ha'\n  exact rank_lt_of_mem y.property"

    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "inductive",
      "name": "Term",
      "statement_lean": "inductive Term (R F : Type) (arity : F → ℕ) : Type\n  | var (name : ℕ)\n  | ordinal (n : ℕ)\n  | atoms\n  | app (f : F) (args : Fin (arity f) → Term R F arity)\n  | pair (a b : Term R F arity)\n  | union (a : Term R F arity)\n  | unique (a : Term R F arity)\n  | card (a : Term R F arity)\n  | equal (a b : Term R F arity)\n  | member (a b : Term R F arity)\n  | isAtom (a : Term R F arity)\n  | input (r : R) (a b : Term R F arity)\n  | not (a : Term R F arity)\n  | and (a b : Term R F arity)\n  | or (a b : Term R F arity)\n  | conditional (c a b : Term R F arity)\n  | comprehend (name : ℕ) (bound guard body : Term R F arity)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "eval",
      "statement_lean": "def eval (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : State F arity A) :\n    Term R F arity → (ℕ → HF A) → HF A\n  | .var n, v => v n\n  | .ordinal n, _ => Hereditary.ordinal n\n  | .atoms, _ => Operational.allAtoms\n  | .app f args, v => s.value ⟨f, fun i => eval rel s (args i) v⟩\n  | .pair a b, v => double (eval rel s a v) (eval rel s b v)\n  | .union a, v => Operational.unionHF (eval rel s a v)\n  | .unique a, v => Operational.uniqueHF (eval rel s a v)\n  | .card a, v => Operational.cardinalityHF (eval rel s a v)\n  | .equal a b, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v = eval rel s b v)\n  | .member a b, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v ∈ eval rel s b v)\n  | .isAtom a, v => Operational.boolean (isSet (eval rel s a v) = false)\n  | .input r a b, v => Operational.boolean (Operational.inputRelation rel r (eval rel s a v) (eval rel s b v))\n  | .not a, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v ≠ Operational.truth)\n  | .and a b, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v = Operational.truth ∧ eval rel s b v = Operational.truth)\n  | .or a b, v => Operational.boolean (eval rel s a v = Operational.truth ∨ eval rel s b v = Operational.truth)\n  | .conditional c a b, v => if eval rel s c v = Operational.truth then eval rel s a v else eval rel s b v\n  | .comprehend n bound guard body, v =>\n      ofFinset (((elements (eval rel s bound v)).filter\n        (fun x => eval rel s guard (Function.update v n x) = Operational.truth)).image\n        (fun x => eval rel s body (Function.update v n x)))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "inductive",
      "name": "Rule",
      "statement_lean": "inductive Rule (R F : Type) (arity : F → ℕ) : Type\n  | skip\n  | update (f : F) (args : Fin (arity f) → Term R F arity) (value : Term R F arity)\n  | parallel (a b : Rule R F arity)\n  | conditional (condition : Term R F arity) (a b : Rule R F arity)\n  | forall (name : ℕ) (bound : Term R F arity) (body : Rule R F arity)\n  | letValue (name : ℕ) (value : Term R F arity) (body : Rule R F arity)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "updates",
      "statement_lean": "def updates [Fintype A] (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : State F arity A) :\n    Rule R F arity → (ℕ → HF A) → Finset (Update F arity A)\n  | .skip, _ => ∅\n  | .update f args value, v => {(⟨f, fun i => (args i).eval rel s v⟩, value.eval rel s v)}\n  | .parallel a b, v => updates rel s a v ∪ updates rel s b v\n  | .conditional c a b, v => if c.eval rel s v = Operational.truth then updates rel s a v else updates rel s b v\n  | .forall n bound body, v => (elements (bound.eval rel s v)).biUnion\n      (fun x => updates rel s body (Function.update v n x))\n  | .letValue n value body, v => updates rel s body (Function.update v n (value.eval rel s v))"
    },

    },

      "name": "step",
      "statement_lean": "def step (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : P.Store (A := A)) : Option (P.Store (A := A)) :=\n  if P.flag s .halt then some s else\n    let u := P.body.updates rel s (fun _ => Operational.empty)\n    if State.Consistent u then some (s.applyUpdates u) else none"
    },

      "name": "run",
      "statement_lean": "def run (rel : R → A → A → Bool) : ℕ → Option (P.Store (A := A))\n  | 0 => some State.initial\n  | j+1 => (run rel j).bind (P.step rel)"
    },
    {

      "name": "active",
      "statement_lean": "def active (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (h : ℕ) : Finset (HF A) :=\n  (Finset.range (h+1)).biUnion (fun j => ((P.run rel j).toFinset).biUnion State.active)"
    },

      "statement_lean": "def Term.roots (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : State F arity A) :\n    Term R F arity → (ℕ → HF A) → Finset (HF A)\n  | t@(.var _), v | t@(.ordinal _), v | t@(.atoms), v => {t.eval rel s v}\n  | t@(.app _ args), v => insert (t.eval rel s v)\n      ((List.ofFn (fun i => roots rel s (args i) v)).foldr (· ∪ ·) ∅)\n  | t@(.pair a b), v | t@(.equal a b), v | t@(.member a b), v\n  | t@(.input _ a b), v | t@(.and a b), v | t@(.or a b), v =>\n      insert (t.eval rel s v) (roots rel s a v ∪ roots rel s b v)\n  | t@(.union a), v | t@(.unique a), v | t@(.card a), v\n  | t@(.isAtom a), v | t@(.not a), v => insert (t.eval rel s v) (roots rel s a v)\n  | .conditional c a b, v => roots rel s c v ∪\n      if c.eval rel s v = Operational.truth then roots rel s a v else roots rel s b v\n  | t@(.comprehend n bound guard body), v => insert (t.eval rel s v)\n      (roots rel s bound v ∪ (elements (bound.eval rel s v)).biUnion (fun x =>\n        roots rel s guard (Function.update v n x) ∪\n        if guard.eval rel s (Function.update v n x) = Operational.truth then\n          roots rel s body (Function.update v n x) else ∅))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Rule.roots",
      "statement_lean": "def Rule.roots (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (s : State F arity A) :\n Rule R F arity → (ℕ → HF A) → Finset (HF A)\n | .skip, _ => ∅\n | .update _ args value, v => value.roots rel s v ∪\n     (List.ofFn (fun i => (args i).roots rel s v)).foldr (· ∪ ·) ∅\n | .parallel a b, v => roots rel s a v ∪ roots rel s b v\n | .conditional c a b, v => c.roots rel s v ∪\n     if c.eval rel s v = Operational.truth then roots rel s a v else roots rel s b v\n | .forall n bound body, v => bound.roots rel s v ∪\n     (elements (bound.eval rel s v)).biUnion (fun x => roots rel s body (Function.update v n x))\n | .letValue n value body, v => value.roots rel s v ∪\n     roots rel s body (Function.update v n (value.eval rel s v))"
    },

    },

      "statement_lean": "def Program.occurring (rel : R → A → A → Bool) (h : ℕ) : Finset (HF A) :=\n P.active rel h ∪ (range h).biUnion (fun j => match P.run rel j with\n   | none => ∅\n   | some s => familyClosure (P.stepRoots rel s))"

    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Choiceless polynomial time with counting does not capture polynomial time](../../preprints/Choiceless-polynomial-time-with-counting-does-not-capture-polynomial-time-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Witnessed symmetric choice is strictly stronger than choiceless polynomial time with counting](../../preprints/Witnessed-symmetric-choice-is-strictly-stronger-than-choiceless-polynomial-time-with-counting-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe formalized result separates polynomial time from choiceless polynomial time with counting. It gives an explicit query on finite structures with eight relations that is invariant under isomorphism and decidable in polynomial time, but is not definable in the stated hereditarily finite-set language with cardinality and polynomial bounds on stages and intermediate objects. The query applies to all finite inputs without a promise. The separate witnessed-symmetric-choice result is not included.\n\nThe formalization proves that witnessed symmetric choice strictly increases the expressive power of choiceless polynomial time with counting. It constructs one fixed sentence with exactly one witnessed-choice occurrence that gives a Boolean answer on every finite input, while no sentence of the original counting formalism defines the same class of inputs."
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that choiceless polynomial time with counting does not capture polynomial time on unordered finite structures, confirming the noncapture conjecture of Blass, Gurevich and Shelah. A linear-consistency query over 𝔽<sub>3</sub> in a fixed binary vocabulary is decidable in polynomial time but not in the full counting formalism.

| [stmt-lem-universe-size](#stmt-lem-universe-size) | lemma | `lem:universe-size` | 4 | For each $L\ge2$, the size of $A_b$ is independent of $b$ and equals |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A query on finite relational structures is a property unchanged by isomorphism. It belongs to polynomial time if a deterministic algorithm decides it in time polynomial in the size of an ordinary encoding of the structure. The algorithm may use the order of that encoding; its answer must be independent of the order. An unordered structure, in contrast, does not supply such an order as one of its relations. Descriptive complexity asks which formalisms can express every polynomial-time query without first choosing an order.

Choiceless polynomial time with counting, denoted $\mathrm{CPT}$ throughout this paper, operates on the hereditarily finite sets built from the input elements as atoms. These are finite sets whose members are atoms or further such sets, with no infinite descending membership chain. The formalism permits set operations, comprehension and the cardinality operation $\mathsf{Card}$, which returns a finite von Neumann ordinal. Each fixed program has polynomial bounds on its computation and on the number of recursively occurring objects. Its operations commute with input automorphisms and do not provide an arbitrary choice of an ordering. We use the established equivalent formulation by interpretation programs; its exact syntax, counting operation and quotient semantics are stated in Section <a href="#sec:coding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coding">6</a>. In particular, $\mathrm{CPT}$ here always includes counting and has no imposed bound on the rank of its sets.

To say that this formalism *captures polynomial time* on unordered finite structures means that every isomorphism-invariant polynomial-time query, in every fixed finite relational vocabulary, is definable by a program of the formalism. We first specify a query on all inputs in a fixed vocabulary.

<a id="sec:query"></a>
#### The separating query

<a id="eq:query-coefficient"></a>
Throughout, let $\mathbb F=\mathbb F_3$. Fix the binary relational vocabulary $$\tau=\{\mathsf{Ed},\mathsf{Cf},\mathsf{EB},\mathsf{VB},
             \mathsf I,\mathsf Z_0,\mathsf Z_1,\mathsf Z_2\}.$$ The subscripts on the last three relation symbols denote elements of $\mathbb F$. For a finite $\tau$-structure $A$, set $$Y=\{y\in A:\mathsf{Ed}(y,y)\},\qquad
 X=\{a\in A:\mathsf{Cf}(a,a)\},\qquad
 X_t=\{a\in X:\mathsf{VB}(t,a)\}\quad(t\in X).$$ No disjointness or equivalence-relation assumption is imposed here. In particular, $X$ and $Y$ may overlap. Define, in $\mathbb F$, $$\label{eq:query-coefficient}
 C(y,a)=\sum_{\substack{x\in Y\\\mathsf I(a,x)}}
          \ \sum_{\substack{\delta\in\mathbb F\\
                              \mathsf Z_\delta(y,x)}}\delta
       \qquad(y\in Y,\ a\in X).$$ If several of the relations $\mathsf Z_\delta(y,x)$ hold, all their contributions are included.

<a id="eq:query-incidence-test"></a>
<a id="eq:query-consistency"></a>
Introduce two separate families of unknowns, $(\lambda_a)_{a\in X}$ and $(\mu_y)_{y\in Y}$. Thus even when $a=y$, $\lambda_a$ and $\mu_y$ are different unknowns. The system defining our query has the equations <a id="eq:query-normalization"></a>$$\label{eq:query-normalization}
 \sum_{a\in X_t}\lambda_a=1\qquad(t\in X)$$ and, for every $(t,y)\in X\times Y$ for which $$\label{eq:query-incidence-test}
 \text{there exist $a\in X_t$ and $x\in Y$ with
          $\mathsf I(a,x)$ and $\mathsf{EB}(y,x)$,}$$ the equation $$\label{eq:query-consistency}
 \mu_y=\sum_{a\in X_t}\lambda_a C(y,a).$$ Let $Q(A)$ mean that this system is consistent over $\mathbb F$. Repeated identical equations are harmless; they remain equations and are not added together. An empty sum is zero, and a system with no equations is consistent. These conventions make $Q$ a query on all finite $\tau$-structures.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Main result

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *The query $Q$ on all finite structures in the fixed vocabulary $\tau$ of eight binary relations is isomorphism-invariant and decidable in deterministic polynomial time, but is not definable in full choiceless polynomial time with counting.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> confirms the noncapture conjecture of Blass, Gurevich and Shelah for the full counting formalism; its historical formulation is discussed below. We first record the ordinary algorithmic upper bound. The rest of the paper proves the choiceless lower bound.

<a id="stmt-prop-query-ptime"></a>
<div id="prop:query-ptime" class="proposition">

**Proposition 2**. *The query $Q$ is isomorphism-invariant and decidable in deterministic polynomial time from an ordered encoding of its input structure.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-query-ptime"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-query-ptime</summary>

*Proof.* An isomorphism carries $X,Y,X_t$ to their counterparts, preserves the coefficient in <a href="#eq:query-coefficient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:query-coefficient">[eq:query-coefficient]</a>, and carries the test <a href="#eq:query-incidence-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:query-incidence-test">[eq:query-incidence-test]</a> to the corresponding test. Consequently it permutes the two families of unknowns and the equations compatibly. Consistency is therefore invariant.

If the input has $n$ elements, there are at most $2n$ unknowns and $n+n^2$ equations. All coefficients and tests can be computed by polynomially many loops over the input universe; for example, a direct implementation uses $O(n^4)$ operations over the fixed field. Gaussian elimination then decides consistency in polynomial time (Shoup 2009, secs. 14.4–14.5) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[shoup2009](#ref-shoup2009)]. Any input order may be used to list the rows and columns: changing it does not change consistency. ◻

</details>

The same query is expressible in two standard linear-algebraic logics. Following Grädel and Pakusa (Grädel and Pakusa 2019, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[graedelPakusa2019](#ref-graedelPakusa2019)], write $\mathrm{FPS}_3$ for fixed-point logic with counting (FPC) extended by the solvability operator over $\mathbb F_3$, and $\mathrm{FPR}_3$ for FPC extended by the rank operator over $\mathbb F_3$. Their two-sorted syntax allows bounded number variables alongside the unordered input elements. We state the consequence for nonempty structures, so that the matrix index sets in its proof are nonempty.

<a id="stmt-cor-linear-algebraic-logics"></a>
<div id="cor:linear-algebraic-logics" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3**. *On nonempty finite $\tau$-structures, $Q$ is definable in both $\mathrm{FPS}_3$ and $\mathrm{FPR}_3$. Consequently neither of these logics is contained in $\mathrm{CPT}$.*

</div>

FPC defines the coefficient predicates and the row test for $Q$. Section <a href="#sec:linear-algebraic-logics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:linear-algebraic-logics">8</a> gives the resulting solvability formula and compares the ranks of the coefficient and augmented matrices. The nonempty witnesses constructed below give both noncontainments.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Background and the obstruction to rank-dependent arguments

The ordered case provides the starting point. Immerman and Vardi showed that first-order logic with a least fixed-point operator expresses exactly the polynomial-time queries on finite structures supplied with a linear order (Immerman 1982; Vardi 1982) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[immerman1982](#ref-immerman1982), [vardi1982](#ref-vardi1982)]. For unordered structures, Chandra and Harel asked whether polynomial-time queries admit an effective enumeration (Chandra and Harel 1982, sec. 5, p. 118) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[chandraHarel1982](#ref-chandraHarel1982)]. Gurevich formulated the question in terms of a logic capturing polynomial time and conjectured that no such logic exists (Y. Gurevich 1988, sec. 7) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[gurevich1988](#ref-gurevich1988)]. Excluding CPT with counting as a candidate leaves this broader question open.

Blass, Gurevich and Shelah introduced choiceless polynomial time to study computation on unordered structures using hereditarily finite sets rather than arbitrary choices of an ordering (Blass, Gurevich, and Shelah 1999) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[bgs1999](#ref-bgs1999)]. Their original 1997 preprint explicitly conjectured that the extension by counting is still a proper fragment of polynomial time (Blass, Gurevich, and Shelah 1999, 1997 version, Introduction, p. 3) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[bgs1999](#ref-bgs1999)]. Their later study of unordered computation examined CFI instances and finite-field linear algebra as possible sources of separation, carefully distinguishing representations with common and independent row and column index sets (Blass, Gurevich, and Shelah 2002, sec. 4 and 6) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[bgs2002](#ref-bgs2002)]. The query here is a particular linear-consistency problem over $\mathbb F_3$, defined without a promise on the input structure.

Shelah’s early work claimed noncapture for counting extensions of choiceless computation (Shelah 2000, Introduction and Section 4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[shelah2000](#ref-shelah2000)]. Later accounts continued to state the capture problem as open, from Blass, Gurevich and Shelah (Blass, Gurevich, and Shelah 2002, sec. 7) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[bgs2002](#ref-bgs2002)] through the 2025 work of Dawar, Grädel, Kullmann and Pago (Dawar et al. 2025, sec. 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[dawarGraedelKullmannPago2025](#ref-dawarGraedelKullmannPago2025)]. We give a separate proof for the explicit query above; Shelah’s claim is not a theorem input to this argument.

The construction of Cai, Fürer and Immerman established the central example of a polynomial-time graph query beyond fixed-point logic with counting (Cai, Fürer, and Immerman 1992) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[cfi1992](#ref-cfi1992)]. Its local gadgets conceal a global parity obstruction from bounded-variable counting formulas. This is an antecedent of our charge-based witnesses, not an identification of those witnesses with the original CFI graphs. The passage from counting logic to choiceless computation is substantial: Dawar, Richerby and Rossman showed that the preordered CFI query obtained from ordered base graphs is definable without counting, while even counting does not suffice if set rank is restricted to $o(\log n/\log\log n)$, where $n$ is the input size (Dawar, Richerby, and Rossman 2008, Theorems 17 and 40, 2007 author version) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[drr2008](#ref-drr2008)]. In particular, their lower bound for every fixed rank cannot by itself yield a separation for the full formalism. Pakusa, Schalthöfer and Selman extended the positive result to connected preordered base graphs whose colour classes have logarithmic size in the number of base vertices (Pakusa, Schalthöfer, and Selman 2016, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[pakusaSchalthoeferSelman2016](#ref-pakusaSchalthoeferSelman2016)]. The preorder orders classes of base vertices, rather than all vertices of the resulting CFI structure.

Positive algebraic results also go beyond the original CFI family. Abu Zaid, Grädel, Grohe and Pakusa obtained choiceless canonization, that is, construction of canonical ordered copies, when a supplied preorder has bounded classes whose induced substructures have Abelian automorphism groups (Abu Zaid et al. 2014, Corollary 19) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[abuZaidGraedelGrohePakusa2014](#ref-abuZaidGraedelGrohePakusa2014)]. Their procedure uses specially structured cyclic systems of equations, not arbitrary unordered linear systems (Abu Zaid et al. 2014, Definition 6 and Theorem 14) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[abuZaidGraedelGrohePakusa2014](#ref-abuZaidGraedelGrohePakusa2014)]. Lichter and Schweitzer obtained analogous canonization for graphs when these induced groups are dihedral or cyclic (Lichter and Schweitzer 2021, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[lichterSchweitzer2021](#ref-lichterSchweitzer2021)]. These results show why the lower-bound argument must exploit the particular action of its nonabelian group, rather than noncommutativity alone.

Functional lower bounds expose different limits. Rossman proved that CPT with counting cannot construct the set of hyperplanes of an input finite vector space over a fixed finite field (Rossman 2010, Theorem 6.1, author version) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[rossman2010](#ref-rossman2010)]. Pago proved that no such program constructs, on every hypercube with $N$ vertices, a total preorder with classes of size $O(\log N)$ (Pago 2021, Theorem 2 and Corollary 3, full version) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[pago2021](#ref-pago2021)]. The latter statement also excludes encodings from which the preorder can be recovered in CPT. It obstructs the route of first constructing a fine preorder and then applying the preordered CFI algorithm. These are functional obstructions, not nondefinability of a Boolean query.

Pago’s symmetric-XOR-circuit approach relates restricted classes of choiceless CFI algorithms to circuit families (Pago 2023, Theorems 2, 31 and 33, full version) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[pago2023](#ref-pago2023)]. The translation permits polynomially many circuit images under automorphisms of the base graph; the circuit lower bound assumes an invariant circuit and different bounds on connections between gates. These hypotheses differ, so that argument does not establish nondefinability for the entire restricted algorithm class.

The support-and-transfer architecture has older roots. Blass, Gurevich and Shelah developed supports and representations by forms evaluated at supporting tuples, called molecules (Blass, Gurevich, and Shelah 1999, secs. 8–9, published version) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[bgs1999](#ref-bgs1999)]. Dawar, Richerby and Rossman developed rank-sensitive support bounds and a counting transfer for hereditarily supported objects (Dawar, Richerby, and Rossman 2008, secs. 7–8, 2007 author version) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[drr2008](#ref-drr2008)]. Using the group-relative homogeneity from Section <a href="#sec:base-counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:base-counting">4</a>, we adapt that representation method in Section <a href="#sec:hf-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hf-transfer">5</a> and prove the required exact counting statements. The support estimate in Section <a href="#sec:support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:support">3</a> instead uses a larger nonabelian group to obtain small supports for its central subgroup, independently of HF rank. Its standard class-two group and alternating-form ingredients, as well as the projection and counting-game methods used for the atom comparison, are credited at their respective proofs. The grid-specific estimate and the quantitative transfer are proved here.

Finally, the connection to arbitrary programs uses the interpretation characterization of Grädel, Pakusa, Schalthöfer and Kaiser (Grädel et al. 2015, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[gkps2015](#ref-gkps2015)]. We use the binary, two-dimensional formulation of Grohe, Schweitzer and Wiebking (Grohe, Schweitzer, and Wiebking 2021, sec. 6, Theorem 18, arXiv version 1) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[gsw2020](#ref-gsw2020)], including its generated-equivalence and existential-representative quotient convention. This characterization is an external input. Section <a href="#sec:coding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coding">6</a> proves the further facts needed here: a polynomial bound on the complete hereditary encoding of a run, and exact formulas for its states whose variable bound is independent of the number of stages. Thus neither a bound on HF rank nor a restriction to a particular algorithmic construction is imposed on the programs excluded by the theorem.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Proof overview

For each side length $L$, we build two structures from the three-dimensional box grid. Each edge carries states consisting of a bounded collection of $\mathbb F_3^2$ face coordinates and one scalar. A vertex configuration chooses incident edge states that agree on their shared face coordinates and whose scalars have a prescribed signed sum, called its charge. Both kinds of states become input atoms, linked only by the fixed binary relations. The two structures have all charges zero, or one charge equal to one. Their size is $N=\Theta(L^3)$. The query’s normalization equations assign field-valued weights summing to one to the configurations at each vertex. Its consistency equations make the weighted scalar value of an edge agree at its two endpoints. Summing the vertex divergence equations then forces the total charge to be zero, since each edge enters one vertex and leaves another. This argument allows arbitrary field-valued weights; it does not assume that a solution selects one configuration per vertex. The all-zero configurations give a solution in the zero-charge structure, so the query has opposite answers on the pair.

The challenge is to show that every fixed full $\mathrm{CPT}$ program eventually gives the same answer on this pair. Three steps connect the symmetries of these atoms to the complete computation.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Rank-independent supports.

The structures have an automorphism group $H$ containing a central subgroup $K$ of divergence-free scalar flows on the edges. A tuple of atoms is a $K$-support of an object if every element of $K$ fixing that tuple also fixes the object. For any fixed $q$, every hereditarily finite object with $H$-orbit of size at most $N^q$ has a support of size $O_q(L(1+\log L)^2)$. The bound does not depend on the object’s rank. Commutators in $H$ force the linear functionals detecting a stabilizer to have nonzero curl on few faces. Subtracting explicitly constructed gradients then concentrates those functionals on few edges. This is the role of the nonabelian group; central flows alone do not supply this argument.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Counting equivalence for all supported objects.

For analysis only, we add a preorder identifying certain classes of atoms and relations comparing scalar differences. These relations are not given to the program. With $M=\lfloor L^{7/4}\rfloor$, the two expanded structures agree on all first-order counting sentences using at most $M$ variable names. A charge discrepancy can be moved along paths in the large component that remains after the edges touched by the assigned atoms are removed. Within either expansion, equality of sufficiently wide counting types also determines the $K$-orbit of a short tuple.

Fix a support length $s=O_q(L(1+\log L)^2)$. Consider all hereditarily finite objects for which the object and every recursive member have a $K$-support of length at most $s$. This is a countably infinite domain of finite objects, containing objects of every finite rank. We represent its objects by finite forms evaluated at supporting atom tuples, then prove equality, membership and exact counting transfer. For each fixed $m$, the two domains agree on all counting sentences with at most $m$ variable names when $L$ is sufficiently large. The key width cost is $O_{q,m}(L^{3/2}(1+\log L)^3)=o(M)$.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### The entire computation fits the comparison.

The interpretation characterization supplies a fixed program whose successive states are quotients of definable pairs from the preceding state. We encode every quotient vertex by a tagged set of ordered pairs of preceding vertex codes. A polynomial run has only polynomially many such codes and recursive constituents; this transitive family is $H$-invariant. The support theorem therefore places the entire encoded trace in the domains just compared.

Finally, each state, halting test and output test has an exact counting-formula definition over its supported domain. Its length may grow with the input, but the number of variable names needed is bounded by a constant depending only on the program. We prove this by a capture-avoiding substitution lemma, including the generated quotient relation and the cardinality comparisons. Choose the program and its constants $q,m$ first, and then take $L$ large. Counting equivalence forces the same halt stage and output on the two structures, contradicting their opposite query answers.

<figure id="fig:proof-map">

<figcaption>The two routes from the input pair. The charge equations give opposite query answers. The symmetry, logic and computation arguments force every fixed choiceless program to give equal answers for sufficiently large grids. The atom expansions are used only for analysis. If the program decided $Q$, its equal outputs would contradict the left-hand route.</figcaption>
</figure>
